37 Neural Vector Embeddings: Dendritic Configurations as Informational
Attractors
Within Super Information Theory (SIT), dendritic structures of neurons serve as biological instantiations of
vector embeddings, dynamically encoding learned statistical distributions of incoming temporal and spatial
coincidence patterns. This process reflects iterative informational synchronization, capturing spatiotemporal
relationships through adaptive dendritic morphology and synaptic connectivity. No conceptual ideas from
the original exposition have been lost; instead, this section now explicitly aligns neural phenomena with
SIT’s informational formalism.
Dendrites function as units of mixed selectivity, responding selectively to complex combinations of inputs
rather than individual stimuli. This selectivity is structurally embedded within dendritic configurations,
manifesting as high-dimensional embeddings of phase wave differentials representing coincident input
patterns. Thus, dendritic morphology physically realizes stable informational attractor states, concretely
encoding experienced patterns into lasting biological forms.
The embedding analogy indexes neuronal phase-wave differentials defined relative to baseline operational
neural synchrony ( Rneural
coh , kept distinct from the spacetime EFT modulus Rcoh = |ψ|/ψ0). Selective
dendritic growth and synaptic pruning implement biological signal filtering that parallels, at a purely
conceptual level, synchronization and dispersion motifs studied in physics.
Memory formation within dendritic architectures can be modeled phenomenologically as stabilization of
low-prediction-error attractors across neuronal populations.
Prospective empirical evaluation of this neurocomputational analogy uses calcium imaging and high-density
electrophysiology to measure dendritic activity and connectivity patterns alongside computational vector-
embedding benchmarks (independently of gravitational EFT).
37.1 Dendritic Architectures as Stored Matrices of Learned Relationships
Viewing dendritic structures simultaneously as vector embeddings and biological lookup tables clarifies
how neurons encode relational memories as matrices of learned statistical regularities. This perspective
integrates structural plasticity mechanisms, including long-term potentiation (LTP) and depression (LTD),
with functional retrieval processes mediated by nonlinear dendritic spikes. Such spikes function analogously
to computational lookup operations, retrieving learned activation patterns corresponding to specific synaptic
inputs.
In operational neural variables (Section 36), dendritic input–output mappings may be parameterized
phenomenologically as

Memory thus becomes a distributed, tensorial phenomenon wherein network-wide dendritic matrices
collectively form higher-dimensional embeddings of complex associative relationships. Such embeddings
encode not merely isolated memories, but comprehensive relational mappings across neuronal populations,
adhering to the following structured analogy:
• Synaptic input patterns : represented as vectors encoding incoming signals.
• Dendritic embedding and retrieval : operationalized through learned matrices encoding relational
mappings from inputs to dendritic outputs.
• Neuronal outputs : resulting vectors composed of graded dendritic signals and spike-induced action
potentials.
• Network-wide memory encoding : represented as higher-order tensors, encompassing distributed
dendritic matrices across multiple neurons.
This rigorous framing clarifies relationships between structural dendritic modifications and functional
synaptic plasticity, uniting neuroscience with formal computational analogies such as vector embeddings,
associative memory, and attention mechanisms. The integration strengthens SIT’s explanatory power,
aligning biological neural structures explicitly with its formal informational dialect.
37.2 Dendritic Vector Embeddings
Biological dendritic structures encode relational memories analogous to high-dimensional tensor
embeddings within artificial neural networks. Viewing each dendrite as a learned multidimensional
associative embedding supplies a motivating neurobiological analogy and proposed representational
correspondence for SIT’s coherence-based modeling, rather than completed biological validation.
38 Phase Wave Differential Tokens and Traveling Waves in Neural
Assemblies
We define the neural phase wave differential token as a deviation ( ∆ ) from the coherent oscillatory pattern
characteristic of a neural array, cluster, cortical column, or other defined neuronal assembly. Mathematically,
this deviation can be represented explicitly as:
Φ token = ∆( φneuron − φgroup)
where φneuron is the instantaneous phase of an individual neuron’s oscillation and φgroup is the mean phase of
its synchronized ensemble.
A spike differing in timing or magnitude from the synchronized ensemble introduces a local phase wave
differential. This initiates traveling waves governed by a diffusion-attenuation equation, now explicitly
defined as: ∂Φ
∂t = D∇2Φ − γΦ
Here, D is the diffusion constant capturing wave propagation across the neural network, and γ characterizes
attenuation through inhibitory interactions and synaptic constraints.

Traveling waves propagate through neural tissue analogously to sequential domino cascades. Each wavefront
activates dendritically embedded vector memories, triggering cascades of neuronal excitation and inhibitory
interactions. Thus, memory retrieval and cognitive processes arise dynamically from these traveling waves.

synchrony Rneural

40 Integrative Insights from Related Frameworks
Super Information Theory (SIT) is not an isolated conceptual structure; rather, it synthesizes key
insights from several complementary theoretical frameworks across quantum physics, gravitational theory,
thermodynamics, and computational neuroscience. Here, we explicitly illustrate how SIT draws upon related
lines of work including Super Dark Time , SuperTimePosition, Micah’ s New Law of Thermodynamics, Self
Aware Networks (SAN) , and Quantum Gradient Time Crystal Dilation within an exploratory conceptual
synthesis spanning quantum, gravitational, and neural analogies.
SIT rigorously synthesizes key prior insights without conceptual loss, including:
• Time-density variations explicitly correlated with quantum coherence-decoherence processes.
• Emergence of gravitational phenomena from coherence-driven thermodynamics, explicitly linked through
local time-density fields.
• Quantum Coherence Coordinates (QCC) introduced as explicit constructs bridging classical informational
theories with contemporary quantum-gravitational concepts.
40.1 Quantum-Gravitational Computational Cycles: Super Dark Time and
SuperTimePosition
The frameworks of Super Dark Time and SuperTimePosition posit deterministic quantum-gravitational
phenomena arising from ultrafast phase oscillations occurring below observational timescales. In
100

--- PAGE 101 ---
SuperTimePosition, quantum entities oscillate deterministically between particle-like localizations and wave-
like delocalizations at extremely high frequencies. Quantum randomness arises observationally due to
synchronization limitations between these rapid internal cycles and slower external measurements.
SIT incorporates these insights explicitly by framing quantum coherence and decoherence as synchronized
and unsynchronized states within informational oscillatory processes. Coherence emerges naturally as stable
synchronization of quantum-gravitational oscillations, whereas decoherence manifests through partial or
incomplete sampling of these deterministic cycles. Hence, quantum randomness and gravitational effects
share a unified deterministic informational foundation.
40.2 Wave-Based Dissipation and Coherence: Micah’s New Law of Thermodynamics
According to Micah’ s New Law of Thermodynamics , physical and informational systems universally tend
toward equilibrium through iterative dissipation of differences, occurring via wave-based phase interactions.
Differences in energy, phase, momentum, or informational content dissipate through iterative exchanges,
driving systems toward synchronized equilibria.
Within SIT, informational coherence directly corresponds to minimal phase differences among quantum-
gravitational oscillators, while decoherence arises from persistent unsynchronized states. Mathematically,
this dissipation-driven synchronization is explicitly described through the coherence–decoherence dynamics:

40.3 Quantum Origins of the Time-Density Field: Super Dark Time
The Super Dark Time framework articulates gravity as emerging directly from local quantum interference
patterns shaping a fundamental quantum-mechanical time-density field. Quantum coherence patterns—
stable interference effects—yield elevated local time densities (gravitational wells). Conversely,

40.4 Predictive Synchrony and Oscillatory Dynamics: Self Aware Networks
The Self Aware Networks (SAN) framework proposes consciousness and cognitive functions as emergent
phenomena arising from multi-scale oscillatory synchrony in neural networks. Neural oscillations encode
predictive coding mechanisms, active inference, and perceptual synchronization, providing robust biological
models for information processing.

40.5 Significance and Interdisciplinary Synthesis
Integrating these insights from Super Dark Time , SuperTimePosition, Micah’ s New Law, and Self Aware

43 Core Predictions and Resolutions to Open Problems
43.1 Physical Grounding for the Free Energy Principle
Super Information Theory provides a candidate field-theoretic underpinning for macroscopic principles of
biological and adaptive self-organization, most notably Karl Friston’s Free Energy Principle (FEP). The FEP
posits that persistent open systems act so as to bound and minimize a variational free-energy functional
serving as an upper bound on sensory surprise. Because FEP is formulated as a substrate-independent
statistical constraint, SIT complements it by specifying a concrete physical mechanism through which free-
energy-like Lyapunov functionals arise in open dissipative media without postulating teleological forces.
Ontological Status of Effective Functionals. No new fundamental “purpose” primitive is introduced into
SAUTH. Any candidate control or memory functional Φ eﬀ is strictly a system-specific reduced effective
functional obtained after coarse-graining open-system environmental couplings in the Feynman–V ernon
influence-functional formalism (Appendices N–P):
• Prediction Error as Phase-Wave Differential: Sensory mismatch corresponds physically to localized
spatial or temporal gradients in (Rcoh, θ) between internal oscillatory assemblies and external boundary
driving.
• Active Inference as Dissipative Coherence Reconfiguration: Open subsystems export entropy into their
environment while relaxing internal phase-wave differentials toward phase-locked attractors, realizing
perception and motor control as damped gradient flows on the reduced informational landscape.
• Stationary Action as the Underlying Law: Apparent free-energy minimization is an emergent
reduced description of trajectories governed at the microscopic level by δSAUTH = 0 coupled to a
thermal/dissipative bath.

47.2 Electron–Beam Deflection Background and Withdrawal of the Legacy CRT Bound
Standard electromagnetic baseline. In a cathode-ray tube (CRT) or an electron microscope, the ordinary

curvature arising in standard quantum mechanics from minimal substitution p → p − eA for a genuine
non-flat electromagnetic connection ( ∇ × A = B ̸= 0).

reinterpreted classical CRT deflection ( v ≃ 2.0 × 107 m s−1, B ≃ 10−2 T, 90◦ bend over L = 5 cm) using
the flat-connection phase-gradient ansatz to quote a heuristic bound λRcoh ≲ 10−8 and describe electron
deflection as a calibrated probe of the coherence field. V ersion 81 explicitly withdraws that CRT-derived
10−8 figure as an empirical SIT bound and withdraws the “calibrated probe” characterization. Because
ordinary Lorentz deflection occurs in a region with B ̸= 0 (which cannot be generated by a smooth pure
gradient ∂µθ) and because neither the visible U (1)EM dictionary ( OPEN-6B) nor the apparatus transfer
function ( OPEN-6D) has been derived from M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1, CRT deflection currently stands solely
as established electromagnetic background rather than an empirical SIT constraint.

embeds alongside or mixes with SIT’s internal U (1) connection (OPEN-6B) and deriving the corresponding
observable shift from the canonical action.

48 Deriving the Standard Model and Gravity from First Principles
Having established the mathematical consistency of Super Information Theory (SIT), this section moves
the theory from phenomenological unification to fundamental explanation. We now examine how Super
Information Theory interfaces with the Standard Model particle spectrum, flavor mixing, and gravitational
coupling, separating rigorously machine-checked Stage-4 chiral-gauge theorems from open topological
research conjectures under QG49 epistemic discipline.
48.1 A Conjectured Topological Particle Spectrum from the Coherence Field
A central ambition of fundamental physics is to derive the observed spectrum of elementary particles
from a minimal set of first principles. Super Information Theory (SIT) proposes a path toward this goal
by conjecturing that elementary particles are stable, quantized topological excitations of the fundamental
complex coherence field, ψ(x). In this framework, the diverse properties of particles—both fermions and
bosons—emerge as calculable invariants of an underlying informational geometry.
48.1.1 Stress-Energy and the Emergence of Gravity
This topological classification is not merely a labeling scheme; it has direct physical consequences for the
spacetime metric. A topological defect in the coherence field carries energy and momentum, and therefore
sources gravitational curvature. Its stress-energy tensor follows directly from varying the SIT master action
with respect to the metric gµν:
T vortex

each stable winding necessarily sources curvature. This leads to a profound conclusion: gravity is not an
extra field but the inevitable geometric response to the energy stored in a coherence defect.
110

--- PAGE 111 ---
48.1.2 The Stability Condition and Informational Structure of Particles
Not all topological configurations are physically realized as stable particles. A vortex must be energetically
stable against decay into radiation. Whether a given vortex preserves macroscopic coherence or dissipates
depends on the balance between its internal gradient energy (informational tension) and the decohering
couplings that radiate energy away. We define the coarse-grained energy per unit length of a defect as:
E = πκc ln(r∞/r0) − Γ, (111)
where r0 is the core radius, r∞ is the system size, and Γ is the dissipation rate determined by environment-
induced decoherence. This leads to two distinct physical regimes:
• E > 0: A coherence-sustaining, long-lived defect . The informational tension dominates, creating a
stable, massive, gravitating particle.
• E < 0: A turbulent, rapidly decaying defect . Dissipation dominates, leading to a transient, radiation-
dominated excitation.
This stability condition is governed by an excitability ratio. Configurations with a low ratio are stable
particles, whereas those with a high ratio are fleeting resonances. Furthermore, the internal structure of
the defect affects the local rate of time via the fundamental SIT constraint ρt = ρ0 exp[αRcoh]. At the
core of a strongly wound vortex, the coherence field must vanish ( Rcoh → 0), causing the time-density to
approach its vacuum value, ρt → ρ0, which dilates proper time. This provides the concrete mechanism by
which coherence defects seed both particle identity and spacetime geometry.
48.1.3 A Unified View of Fermions and Bosons: Sources and Interactions
SIT makes a fundamental distinction between the two classes of elementary particles, grounding it in their
distinct informational roles.
• Fermions as Stable Topological Sources: The fundamental particles of matter (quarks and leptons) are
identified as stable, localized, and topologically protected defects (solitons, vortices, or knots) in the
coherence field. Their persistence is guaranteed by topological invariants. They are the stable sources
of informational structure in the universe. The Pauli Exclusion Principle arises as a topological constraint
preventing two identical defects from coexisting at the same location.
• Bosons as Quantized Propagators of Interaction: The fundamental force carriers are identified not
as stable defects, but as the quantized, propagating excitations of the fields that mediate interactions
between the fermionic sources. They are the carriers of informational change, phase communication, and
topological reconfiguration.
48.1.4 Topological Classification of Fermion Families and Generations
The classification of fermionic matter rests on the topological properties of the coherence manifold, Mcoh,
which represents the space of all possible stable configurations of the ψ field.
• Particle Families as Homotopy Classes: The primary division of fermions into distinct families is
determined by the topology of Mcoh. We postulate that different particle families correspond to elements
of different homotopy groups, πn(Mcoh). For instance, the fundamental group π1(Mcoh) might classify
leptonic states, while a higher-order group like π3(Mcoh) could correspond to the more complex internal
structure of quarks.
111

--- PAGE 112 ---
• Generations as Nested Topological Structures: The existence of three generations of matter is proposed
to arise from higher-order, nested topological structures. A second-generation particle would be a stable
topological excitation of the field configuration that constitutes a first-generation particle. The increasing
mass of higher generations is a natural consequence of the increased gradient energy and informational
complexity required to sustain these nested configurations.
48.1.5 Quantum Numbers and Bosons from Geometric Principles
Within SIT, conserved quantum numbers and their associated force-carrying bosons are not abstract labels
but emerge from the geometry of the coherence field and its phase.
• Electromagnetism (Canonical Gauge Boundary, OPEN-6B): In the canonical action
M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1, local U (1) gauge invariance is carried by an independent internal connection
Aµ (distinct from a flat phase gradient ∂µθ), which acquires a broken-phase mass m2
A = κc ˆq2v2
0/fEM0
whenever ˆqv0 ̸= 0 . Identifying the massless visible photon therefore cannot rely on taking Aµ ∝ ∂µθ
or equating the broken internal connection directly with visible electromagnetism; instead, it requires
either a separate unbroken visible U (1)EM gauge field decoupled from the broken coherence vacuum
expectation value v0 or an explicit visible-versus-hidden gauge dictionary ( OPEN-6B, with tree-level
kinetic mixing analyzed in Stage 6).
• Strong Force: The SU (3) color charge of quarks emerges from a more complex, non-Abelian topological
structure. The gluons are the eight quantized excitations of the connections on this non-Abelian structure,
mediating transitions between the three stable color states.
• Weak Force: The weak force changes a particle’s topological identity. The massive W and Z bosons are
high-energy, propagating excitations of the coherence field capable of inducing topological reconnections,
thereby transforming one type of fermionic knot into another. Their large mass reflects the high energy
barrier for this fundamental reconfiguration.
48.1.6 Spin and Topological Statistics
The distinction between fermions and bosons is encoded in the behavior of their corresponding topological
excitations under a 2π rotation. Fermionic modes are identified with field configurations that acquire a
non-trivial phase factor (a minus sign) upon such a rotation (e.g., a skyrmion or a twisted loop), naturally
embedding the spin-statistics theorem into the geometry of the coherence field.
48.1.7 The Origin of Mass Scale and the Higgs Boson from the SIT Vacuum
The parameters that govern mass are determined by the self-consistent properties of the SIT vacuum. The
informational potential V (|ψ|, ρt) possesses a non-trivial minimum that defines the vacuum state, and the
curvature of this potential sets the fundamental energy scale for all excitations. Therefore, mass does not
arise from coupling to a separate Higgs field, but from the energy required to ”lift” a topological excitation
out of the stable SIT vacuum.
In this picture, the experimentally observed Higgs boson is reinterpreted. It is not a quantum of a separate,
fundamental field. Instead, we propose it is a massive, scalar, non-topological excitation of the SIT vacuum
field itself . It is a propagating ripple in the background ”informational stiffness” of spacetime. This
excitation naturally couples to the topological defects (fermions), affecting their stability and energy, thereby
reproducing the observed Higgs mechanism without requiring an additional fundamental field.
112

--- PAGE 113 ---
48.1.8 Roadmap to a Unique and Complete Spectrum
This framework moves beyond analogy to a concrete program of proof. The ultimate goal is to demonstrate
that the complete set of stable, low-energy topological solutions to the non-linear SIT field equations is
precisely the Standard Model particle spectrum, with no extraneous states. This program contrasts with
purely algebraic approaches by being fundamentally geometric and dynamic. The roadmap to this proof
involves four key steps:
1. Define the full non-linear field equations for the complex field ψ and ρt, including the self-interaction
terms specified by the informational potential V (|ψ|, ρt).
2. Analyze the stability of solitonic solutions to these equations, identifying which configurations are
energetically stable and long-lived.
3. Classify all stable solutions using the tools of algebraic topology (homotopy, homology, and knot theory)
to generate a complete set of topological invariants.
4. Prove a one-to-one correspondence between this set of classified topological excitations and the
observed particles of the Standard Model, and demonstrate that no other stable solutions are permitted in
the low-energy regime.
If successful, this research program would reframe the Standard Model of particle physics not as a collection
of fundamental postulates, but as the potential emergent consequence of a deeper, unified informational
geometry.
48.2 A Proposed Mechanism for Mass and Particle Mixing from Informational Stability
Following the conjecture that particles are stable, topological excitations of the coherence field, we now
outline a proposed mechanism for explaining their fundamental properties: mass and mixing. This
section conjectures that the observed mass hierarchy and mixing matrices of the Standard Model are not
arbitrary parameters but could be emergent consequences of the stability and geometry of these underlying
informational modes.
48.2.1 The Mass-Resilience Relation and the Informational Resilience Index

50.1 Halfway Universe Heuristic and Sign Correction of the Contrast Integral
Conceptually, the “Halfway Universe” proposal investigates whether cosmic structure formation exhibits
a dynamical balance between compact high-density gravitational regions (black holes and clusters) and
expanding under-dense cosmic voids, motivating open questions about Bekenstein–Hawking entropy
bookkeeping and late-time cosmic acceleration (without claiming to resolve the black-hole information
paradox or derive a conserved cosmic coherence budget from M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1).
Sign Correction of the Earlier Zero-Sum Equation. Earlier drafts wrote
∫

α

Rcoh ≥ 0 everywhere and the void limit sets Rcoh → 0 and ρt → ρt,0, voids contribute 0 (not negative
values) to the integral, so cancellation cannot occur as written. Formulating a genuine zero-mean cosmic
contrast requires defining a signed fluctuation relative to a specified spatial mean background ¯Rbg(t) :=
⟨Rcoh⟩V (t) > 0,
δRcoh(x, t) := Rcoh(x, t) − ¯Rbg(t),

construction; whether any nonlinear solution of a cosmological SIT action linksδρt linearly to δRcoh remains
an open Level-1 problem.

51.3 Why Sharp Spatial Mode Cutoffs PN Break Boost Covariance in 4D Gauge Loops
(hexagon:2611.00175v1)

perturbative analysis of 4D gauge theory on R × S3 (The Hawking–Page transition at weak coupling ,

due to three distinct loop-level obstructions:

(∇ · A = 0 ) generates the nonlocal Coulomb interaction HC = g2

local counter-potentials VCL required to preserve Ward identities.
2. Quadratic Scalar Mass Counterterms from Boost Boundary Violations: Because Lorentz and
conformal boost generators couple adjacent harmonic shells n ↔ n + 1, truncating sharply at n ≤ J

54 Comparative Synthesis: Dated 2025 SIT Gauge-Holonomy Anchors vs.

Milestones
Between February–December 2025 and October–November 2026, two independent research streams
converged on compact-group holonomy, lattice admissibility, and machine-checked Lean 4 verification in
four-dimensional gauge physics. Adhering strictly to QG49 candor, we record both the documented 2025
SIT archival anchors and the sharp axiomatic distinctions separating modified-action coherence gating from
unmodified pure 4D Y ang–Mills Wightman/OS existence.

mostly-plus relativistic convention (−, +, +, +) with Minkowski metric ηµν = diag( −1, +1, +1, +1) and
natural units ℏ = c = 1 ([M ] = GeV) unless SI factors are explicitly restored.

A.1 Addressing Potential Criticisms and Commitment Levels
To prevent conflating core laboratory predictions with exploratory extensions, Super Information Theory is
organized into distinct levels of empirical and mathematical commitment (refined further into the five-tier
formal ledger QG49 in Appendix Z.5):
• Level 0 (Core EFT and Non-Perturbative Lattice Dynamics): The canonical action
M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1 (E ≪ Λ UV), its curved 3 + 1 D ADM constraint closure, its 4D Haar
Wilson-coherence lattice realization, and its laboratory falsifiers in 87Rb Bose–Einstein condensates,
optical/nuclear clocks, and atom interferometers. Empirical exclusion of all viable screened windows at
Level 0 falsifies the core physical theory.
• Level 1 (Cosmological and Interdisciplinary Regimes): Applications of (Rcoh, ρt) gradients to galactic
rotation curves, weak-lensing shear residuals, Hubble-tension sampling, and mesoscopic cortical phase-
synchronization dynamics. These models are constrained by Level 0 bounds but are modular: revising a
cosmological or neural parameter fit does not invalidate Level 0.
143

--- PAGE 144 ---
• Level 2 (Conditionally Isolated Arithmetic Spectral Explorations): Operator-theoretic comparisons
with zeta-function spectra, formally decoupled from the physical SIT core under ODQ-7 (HELD_PER_ODQ7,
rh_solved = false ).
A.2 Summary
V ariations ofSAUTH in (−, +, +, +) generate ghost-free, positive-energy scalar, gauge, and metric dynamics
that reduce smoothly to General Relativity, Maxwell electrodynamics, quantum mechanics, and Boltzmann
kinetic theory in tested limits while yielding crisp laboratory falsification targets.

C Chronology of Core Concepts and Bounded Comparison with Causal
Fermion Systems
This appendix records the dated public timeline of high-level conceptual framing elements in the SIT
precursor corpus (2017–2022) alongside later expositions in Causal Fermion Systems (CFS, 2024–2025).
Consistent with Paper 048 ( Causal Fermion Systems and Super Information Theory , July 28, 2026) and
Appendix X, this chronological comparison documents narrative-level conceptual parallels only; it does not
claim mathematical equivalence, semantic embedding, or independent empirical validation of SIT.
C.1 Phase 1: Origination of Conceptual Framing in SIT’s Precursors (2017–2022)
The precursor work for SIT explored relational descriptions of information, observation, and time density
across neuroscience and physics:
2017 (Neural Lace Podcast): Public expositions introduced two high-level conceptual motifs:
145

--- PAGE 146 ---
• Relational Information: Framing operational bits around relational “coincidence patterns” of events
rather than isolated static states.
• Distributed Internal Observation: The NAPOT (Neural Array Projection Oscillation Tomography)
narrative framing internal observation via distributed internal synchronization without an external
observer.
2022 (Quantum Gradient Time Crystal Dilation - QGTCD): Precursor physical drafts introduced a
scalar time-density concept ( ρt) and explored gradient-driven gravitational analogies.
C.2 Phase 2: The State of Causal Fermion Systems (Pre-2024)
In its earlier technical expositions (e.g. 2015 overviews), CFS formulated spacetime primarily as the support
of a universal measure ρ on abstract operator spaces, while conjecturing that the causal action principle might
account for state reduction.
C.3 Phase 3: Later CFS Expositions and Bounded Comparison Status (2024–2025)
During 2024–2025, CFS publications developed operator-algebraic accounts of “effective dynamical
collapse” (2024) and expository descriptions of spacetime as a “web of correlations” of a quantum many-
body system (2025). While these motifs echo the 2017 relational vocabulary at a high-level narrative
plane, Paper 048 establishes that the only controlled mathematical overlap verified to date is a shared
regulated Einstein–Dirac effective-equation limit against July 2026 V ersion 80 Draft 13—which matches an
ordinary Einstein–Dirac null control with zero discriminatory value—and extending that truncation check to
November 2026 M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1 remains a separate open calculation (Appendix X).

Summary of Reductions and Open Microscopic Bridges
Gravity Reduction Constant vacuum fields (ρ0, v0) reduce the metric equation algebraically to Einstein’s
equations (exact in source-free vacuum; exact sourced GR+Maxwell in the unbroken limitv0 = 0; controlled
asymptotic approximation under heavy-scalar decoupling).
Decoupled QFT Limit In flat spacetime with decoupled scalar fluctuations, matter and gauge sectors retain
standard Minkowski QFT dynamics up to Λ UV-suppressed EFT operators.
Kinetic Correspondence Standard Boltzmann and Navier–Stokes equations hold in the decoupled
background under their established statistical-mechanical hypotheses, while SIT-specific kinetic collision
and transport derivations remain open.
Scope Note: Rather than claiming complete first-principles derivation of kinetic theory or quantum
measurement from classical scalar fields, V ersion 81 establishes exact algebraic/unbroken/decoupled
consistency with General Relativity and perturbative QFT while keeping microscopic kinetic and
measurement bridges explicitly open.

E.6 Retirement of Legacy Parameter Boxes and the OPEN-6 Likelihood Program
Earlier drafts of this appendix listed illustrative phenomenological boxes ( 10−18 eV ≲ mρ, mR ≲ 10−3 eV,
10−4 ≲ λ ≲ 10−2, and |δRcoh| ≲ 10−5) and asserted that those ranges ensured consistency with Solar
System, laboratory, cosmological, and quantum-coherence tests. V ersion 81 explicitly retires all such
ungrounded parameter boxes as empirical conclusions for three reasons:
1. Missing Action-to-Charge-to-Apparatus Map (OPEN-6): Converting primary Solar System, torsion-
balance, optical-clock, or cold-atom datasets into valid exclusion contours on M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1
requires deriving microscopic material scalar charges (OPEN-6A), the visible U (1)EM photon dictionary
(OPEN-6B), laboratory preparation sources Jprep (OPEN-6C), and full apparatus transfer functions
(OPEN-6D), subject to the mass-calibration and form-factor identifiability controls of Section 28.4.
2. Field-Normalization Convention Dependence of Kinetic Coefficients: Bounding a standalone
kinetic coefficient (such as λ or κR) without fixing the field normalization and the remaining couplings
in the action is convention-dependent, because rescaling a scalar field absorbs kinetic normalization
into its mass and interaction couplings (as formalized by D = diag(1, ψ0) in Subsection E.5).
3. Heavy Decoupled Benchmarks Lie Far Outside the Legacy Box: Canonical benchmark
BM_PHYS_EFT_1 places the scalar eigenmasses at m1 ≈ 2.45 × 1014 GeV and m2 ≈ 2.83 × 1016 GeV
(λ1 ≈ 8.056 × 10−31 m), demonstrating that heavy, radiatively stable regimes lie over 25 orders of
magnitude above the legacy 10−3 eV box while remaining strictly non-excluded under the conditional
dust closure of Section 52.4.
Archival exploratory scales from earlier drafts are therefore retained solely as historical context; quantitative
empirical bounds belong exclusively to the conditional response and primary-provenance likelihood program
documented in Section 52.4 and primary_measurements_manifest_v2.json.

F.2 2. Projective Qubit Measurement vs. Relative Phase Resetting: Worked Example
We summarize standard projective measurement for a two-level quantum system while explicitly correcting

Why Fixing Relative Phase θ ∈ { 0, π} Does Not Project onto |0⟩ or |1⟩: Earlier drafts wrote that

(2|ρ01| = 1 )! Resetting a relative phase unitary-rotates a superposition on the Bloch equator; it never
collapses the diagonal populations to (1, 0) or (0, 1).

H Computational Methods, Simulations, and Pseudocode
This appendix provides exploratory computational methodologies, pseudocode, and simulation sketches
intended for prospective numerical modeling and future empirical testing of SIT hypotheses once
microphysical source and detector mappings ( OPEN-6) are derived.
Computational methodologies
Predictive modelling uses three complementary numerical strategies:
156

--- PAGE 157 ---
Finite-element integration. Equation set ( 8)–(33) is discretised on unstructured tetrahedral meshes with
adaptive refinement at steep coherence gradients. A Crank–Nicolson scheme advances ρt and Rcoh
implicitly, conserving the energy to better than 10−7 per timestep while capturing kilometric oscillations
in interferometry test-beds.
Agent-based kinetic Monte Carlo. Microscopic “agents” carry local phase θi and perform collisional
updates θi ↦→ θi + ( θj − θi) exp(−∆ /τdec), where τdec derives from ( 84). Averaging over 109 agents
reproduces the continuum limit and reveals self-organised synchrony waves analogous to cortical gamma
bursts and spiral density waves in protoplanetary disks.
Quantum/classical hybrid circuits. High-coherence patches are evolved on NISQ hardware via Suzuki–
Trotter factorisation of the Rcoh Hamiltonian, while low-coherence regions follow classical Langevin
dynamics. A reversible SW AP gate bridges the two domains every micro-iteration, ensuring phase continuity
across the quantum–classical interface.
H.1 Finite Element SIT Solver Pseudocode
Finite element methods (FEM) are employed for solving SIT’s coupled PDEs numerically. Pseudocode
structure:
initialize_grid(domain_size, resolution)
initialize_fields(rho_t, R_coh, boundary_conditions)
while time < simulation_time:
compute_gradients(R_coh, rho_t)
update_fields_via_PDEs(R_coh, rho_t, dt)
apply_boundary_conditions(fields)
save_output(fields)
H.2 Monte Carlo Approaches
Monte Carlo methods capture informational decoherence processes probabilistically:
for each simulation_step:
sample_initial_conditions(R_coh_distribution)
propagate_information_via_MonteCarlo(fields)
evaluate_statistics_and_uncertainties(fields)
record_results()
H.3 Hybrid Quantum-Classical Pathway Simulations
Hybrid models integrate classical gravitational dynamics with quantum informational coherence:
157

--- PAGE 158 ---
initialize_classical_gravity_fields(g_mu_nu)
initialize_quantum_coherence_fields(R_coh)
for each timestep:
evolve_quantum_coherence(R_coh)
feed_coherence_back_to_classical(g_mu_nu, R_coh)
update_classical_fields_via_GR(g_mu_nu)
iterate_until_convergence()
These computational approaches facilitate empirical testing and refinement of SIT predictions.
I Calibration Pipeline across Metrology and Neuroscience
Coefficients of Φ teleo and small SIT couplings (γ, χ) are first constrained by optical-clock residuals
(Appendix A), then refined using neural coherence datasets that estimate ⟨Rcoh⟩ dynamics under volitional
tasks, and finally propagated into the BEC benchmark (Appendix K) and the three-path prediction (Sec. M)
for parameter continuity across domains.

thermal cloud.

P Existence and Uniqueness of Contractive Fixed Points
Let X be a Banach space of reduced states Σ and R : X → X a Fréchet-C1 coarse-grained return map. If
there exists k ∈ (0, 1) with
∥R(x) − R(y)∥X ≤ k ∥x − y∥X ∀x, y ∈ X,
then by Banach’s fixed-point theorem R admits a unique fixed point Σ ⋆, with operator norm ∥DR|Σ ⋆∥ ≤
k < 1, spectral radius ρ[DR|Σ ⋆] ≤ k < 1, and zero clipped attenuation cost C+
rec[Σ ⋆] =

Numerics with a linear Φ( t) ramp show quantized steps in ν, dips in minx |ψ| preceding each step, and

Minimal pseudocode for reproducibility:
164

--- PAGE 165 ---
Initialize grid x in [0, L), time step dt, total steps.
Set parameters D, \mu, g, \omega_0, \eta, \Omega, noise_amp.
Initialize \psi(x,0) = small-amplitude complex noise.
For each step n:
t \leftarrow n\cdot dt
A \leftarrow \Phi(t)/L (with \Phi ramped from 0 \to 2\pi)
Compute \psi_x via centered difference; \psi_xx likewise.
cov_lap \leftarrow \psi_xx - 2iA \psi_x - A^2 \psi
nonlin \leftarrow (\mu - g|\psi|^2) \psi + i\omega_0 \psi
drive \leftarrow \eta exp(i\Omega t)
noise \leftarrow complex Gaussian, var \propto noise_amp/sqrt(dt)
\psi \leftarrow \psi + dt*[D*cov_lap + nonlin + drive] + noise*dt
\theta_unwrap \leftarrow unwrap(arg \psi along x)
\nu(t) \leftarrow round((\theta_unwrap(L) - \theta_unwrap(0))/2\pi)
amin(t) \leftarrow min_x |\psi|
j \leftarrow Im( \psi^* (\psi_x - iA \psi) )
C(t) \leftarrow sum j\cdot dx
Save \nu(t), amin(t), C(t), and snapshots of \theta_unwrap, |\psi|.
Here, C(t) = ∑j dx is the integrated current, ν(t) is the winding number, and amin(t) is the minimum
amplitude.

In plain terms, this toy simulation illustrates how a driven damped 1D ring can execute discrete phase-slip

from M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1.

V Super Information Theory (SIT) Is Constructed Atop a Physical
Mechanism—SuperTimePosition
SuperTimePosition (STP) supplies the physical mechanism that renders a local, deterministic, coherence-
based field ontology compatible with quantum correlations inside Super Information Theory. Here we state
(i) the minimal physical requirements of STP , (ii) the exact manner in which STP interfaces with Bell’s
theorem, and (iii) its falsifiable experimental signatures.
V .1 Local Determinism and What Bell Actually Constrains
Bell’s theorem constrains hidden-variable completions under the joint conjunction of two distinct hypotheses:
167

--- PAGE 168 ---
1. Local Response (Parameter Locality): Outcomes at wing A and wing B depend solely on the local
analyzer setting and local hidden state, A = A(a, λloc) and B = B(b, λloc), with zero superluminal
causal influence.
2. Measurement Independence (Statistical Setting Independence): The hidden-state distribution at
emission is statistically independent of the analyzer configurations, ρ(λ | a, b) = ρ(λ).
STP introduces a locally real, ultrafast cycling phase variable λ(t) whose coarse-grained residue λ mod 2 π
is undersampled by slower macroscopic detectors, explaining why individual quantum outcomes appear
probabilistic despite deterministic phase-gate dynamics. Crucially, however, determinism alone does not
evade Bell: retaining both local response and measurement independence cannot reproduce the full − cos θ
singlet correlation curve.
In the SIT+STP framework, singlet correlations arise by physically relaxing measurement independence via
causal common-past field consistency: detector analyzer configurations are physical matter/electromagnetic
distributions whose world-tubes inhabit a shared backward light cone with the emission source, sourcing
retarded perturbations in (ρt, ψ) across their common causal past so that
ρ(λ | a, b) ̸= ρ(λ). (164)
Strictly retarded hyperbolic propagation ( cchar = 1 ) and local outcome response are preserved throughout,
with zero superluminal signaling and zero retrocausality.
V .2 Local Entanglement via Phase-Locking and Operational No-Signaling
Entangled pairs emerge from common emission events with phase-locked internal cycles, while shared past
field history biases ρ(λ | a, b). Crucially, operational no-signaling is enforced by rotational and π-inversion
symmetries of the stationary phase distribution: integrating over the remote outcome leaves single-wing
marginals strictly independent of the remote setting, P (A | a, b) = P (A | a) = 1 /2 and P (B | a, b) =
P (B | b) = 1 /2, while joint expectations reproduce E(a, b) = − cos(θa − θb) in the strong-locking limit.
V .3 Unified Wave Mechanics from Quantum to Cosmic Scales
The same dynamical primitives—phase oscillation, synchronization, and relaxation under gradients ofρt and
Rcoh—operate from microscopic interferometers to collective condensed-matter and astrophysical structures,
controlled by coupling scales and environmental decoherence rates.
V .4 Testable and Falsifiable Predictions of the STP Mechanism
• Attosecond and Zeptosecond Phase Substructure: Probing state transitions at sampling bandwidths
approaching internal phase-cycle timescales predicts structured sub-cycle beat modulations rather than
featureless white randomness.
• Gravitational / Time-Density Visibility Tuning: Entanglement visibility across baselines spanning
distinct gravitational potentials is predicted to exhibit small, systematic ρt-dependent phase-steering shifts.
• Finite-Locking Visibility Ceiling: Away from asymptotic strong phase-locking, the steering-to-noise
ratio simultaneously fixes both deviations from − cos θ and the maximal achievable CHSH parameter

exempting them from causal field equations.

V .7 Fast-Switching Bell Tests and the Physical Meaning of “Setting”
In loophole-reduced fast-switching Bell experiments, an analyzer “setting” a comprises the physical world-
tube of the random-number generator, switching optoelectronics, and analyzer crystal. Because those
physical degrees of freedom evolve deterministically from the overlap of the backward light cones of wings
A, B, and the source, strictly retarded field propagation through the shared causal past remains causally
sufficient without invoking advanced waves, provided local marginals respect operational no-signaling.
V .8 Integration into the Unified Variational Framework

X Bounded Comparison with Causal Fermion Systems and Open
Formalization Program
Consistent with Paper 048 ( Causal Fermion Systems and Super Information Theory , July 28, 2026),
V ersion 81 replaces earlier assertions of “independent validation,” “semantic embedding,” “formal
operational equivalence,” and “greater expressive power” with a strict separation between: (1) dated
narrative-level conceptual parallels, (2) Paper 048’s bounded effective-equation comparison against an
ordinary Einstein–Dirac null control, and (3) an explicitly unproved proposed categorical and process-
calculus formalization program.
X.1 Bounded Chronological and Effective-Equation Status (Paper 048)
Public precursors in the SIT archive (2017 Neural Lace Podcast and NAPOT notes; 2022 QGTCD notes)
introduced relational “coincidence-pattern” and distributed internal-observation vocabulary prior to 2024–
2025 Causal Fermion Systems (CFS) expositions describing “effective dynamical collapse” and spacetime as
a “web of correlations.” However, sharing high-level relational vocabulary is not an independent empirical
or mathematical validation of SIT.
At the mathematical equation level, Paper 048 establishes a narrow, bounded comparison using frozen
sources: under its regulated constant-coupling continuum assumptions, the CFS field equations reduce to
Einstein gravity coupled to one on-shell Dirac excitation, matching both a specialized constant-background
neutral-Dirac submodel of July 2026 V ersion 80 Draft 13 and a direct Einstein–Dirac null control. Because
all three share the same Einstein–Dirac equation class, that overlap carrieszero discriminatory value against
the ordinary Einstein–Dirac null and does not map primitive CFS operator measures to SIT fields. Testing
whether a consistent constant-background truncation of November 2026 M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1 admits the
same Einstein–Dirac sector remains a separate open adapter calculation.
X.2 Proposed Categorical and Process-Calculus Formalization Program (All Constructions
Open)
Earlier drafts sketched a categorical comparison ( Φ : CCFS → C SIT) and a two-outcome process-calculus
comparison over a finite observational fragment G = (O, τ, G, T ) while prematurely describing the desired
conclusions (semantic embedding, weak bisimulation, and non-existence of a reverse functor) as already
demonstrated. V ersion 81 explicitly demotes those statements to proposed open mathematical work :
1. Why the Two-Outcome Process Sketch Does Not Establish Operational Equivalence: Assigning
the identical abstract transition expression P = τ.(obs(o1).0 + obs(o2).0) to both CFS and SIT proves
only equality of those two assigned expressions; it does not derive that labeled transition system from
either the CFS causal action principle or M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1.
2. Outstanding Obligations Required for Any Future Categorical Theorem (T1–T5): Any future
categorical embedding or separation claim would require explicitly constructing and proving all five
currently unproved obligations: ( T1) concrete definitions of the open-system categories CCFS and CSIT

directly from the actual physical equations (not postulated labels); ( T2) explicit derivation of the black-
box functors to LinRel and proof of natural isomorphism α; (T3) explicit construction of Φ with proved

Appendix AB: Five-Tier Epistemic Ledger ( QG49), M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1
Benchmarks, and Stage 1–6 Lean 4.30 Theorem Index
To enforce uncompromising scientific transparency across mathematical proofs, perturbative effective field
theory, numerical lattice simulations, retired historical hypotheses, and open mathematical and observational
problems, this appendix records the complete V ersion 81 five-tier epistemic ledger ( QG49), the machine-
precision numerical benchmark suite (M_AUTH_SIT_EFT_v1 canon v1_1), and the complete Stage 1–6 (plus
three publication addenda) Lean 4.30 module and capstone theorem index. Each formal theorem addresses
a specific bounded mathematical claim; aggregate module or theorem counts never substitute for unresolved
microphysical derivations or quantum continuum limits ( OPEN-1–OPEN-6).

Inspirations
For the 41st draft of the paper. The author thanks Y u Deng, Zaher Hani, and Xiao Ma for their groundbreaking
work rigorously deriving macroscopic irreversibility from classical microscopic dynamics. In addition public
discussions on social networks by many individuals have inspired the new clarification on the gauge-theoretic
structure of quantum phase, and the new Network Formulation of Super Information Theory.
The development and empirical framing of Super Information Theory (SIT) has been enriched by
interdisciplinary dialogue with the broader communities of mathematical physics, neuroscience, and artificial
intelligence, including open discussions on preprint servers and science journalism platforms. The author
acknowledges the influence of foundational works in quantum information, gauge theory, thermodynamics,
and neural computation, as cited throughout the text.
Constructive feedback from online forums, technical correspondents, and peer reviewers has greatly
contributed to the rigor and clarity of this manuscript. Any errors or omissions are the sole responsibility of
the author.
